I'm definitely still there, just... empty. Everything around me is still happening. I can hear the cars, the wind... especially the wind. It's been so windy lately. The other day, it blew my neighbor's recycling bin all the way down the street, and all these empty soda cans were just rolling everywhere. He drinks so much Diet Coke, it's unbelievable. I tried telling him about the aspartame, you know, how bad it is for you, but he just laughed and said it's his only vice. He's a really nice guy, though, always shovels my driveway when it snows. He has one of those really nice snowblowers, the red ones with the heated handles. I've always wanted one of those. Shoveling by hand is just so hard on my back, especially since I'm on my feet all day at work anyway. But yeah, no, things don't seem distant or unreal. I'm just standing there, and my brain is just... off.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any numbness, tingling, weakness, or difficulty moving any part of your body during or around the time your mind goes blank?\n\nPatient: No, I don't get any numbness or tingling. My body works fine, it's just my brain that stops. I mean, my feet ache, but that's just from standing on the pavement for hours. You really need good insoles if you're going to be a crossing guard. I bought these gel ones last month from the pharmacy, the one on the corner of 5th and Main? They have this huge display of them right when you walk in. I actually went in there just to get some cough drops because the exhaust fumes from the cars were making my throat scratchy. They had cherry and honey-lemon, and I always pick honey-lemon because cherry reminds me of that awful liquid medicine my mom used to make me take when I was a kid. It came in this dark brown glass bottle and tasted like thick, sweet plastic. I used to try and spit it into the sink when she turned around, but she always caught me because it stained the porcelain bright red. It took forever to scrub out, she had to use bleach and everything. The smell of bleach always gives me a headache now. But, um... no, no weakness or anything like that. Just the blanking out.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever noticed any changes in your vision, like blurring, double vision, or parts of your visual field missing, when your mind goes blank?\n\nPatient: No, my vision is fine. I don't get blurry or see double or anything like that. I can see everything perfectly clear, right down to the little pebbles on the street. Actually, the other day I was looking down and I saw this shiny thing in the gutter, and I thought it was a quarter. I bent down to pick it up, and it was just a stupid bottle cap. A silver one, from one of those fancy glass soda bottles. People just throw their trash everywhere, it's so disrespectful. The litter by the crosswalk is terrible. I'm always picking up fast food wrappers and empty cigarette packs. The worst is the chewing gum, though. It gets stuck to the bottom of my boots, those same boots I was telling you about, and it's impossible to scrape off. I tried using a butter knife once, and I ended up snapping the handle right off. It was one of my good knives, too, from the set my sister got me for my birthday a few years ago. She always buys me kitchen stuff, even though I don't really cook that much. I mostly just microwave things or make sandwiches. I make a really good grilled cheese, though. The secret is to use mayonnaise on the outside of the bread instead of butter. It makes it so much crispier. My mom taught me that trick. She used to make us grilled cheese and tomato soup every time it rained. But... yeah, no, my vision doesn't change at all when my mind stops. I can see just fine.\n\nDoctor: While your mind is blank, do you lose awareness of what’s happening around you, or does everything remain clear and understandable even though your thoughts are gone?\n\nPatient: Well, like I said, everything around me is still clear. I'm aware of it all. It's just my thoughts that vanish. That's actually why I came in, because my mind keeps going blank at the worst times. Like, I'll look at the road-closure forms or my stop-slow paddle, and I suddenly cannot remember the steps I've done for years. It's so embarrassing. Especially with the paddle, because the drivers are looking right at you, waiting to see if they can go. Some of them get so impatient, honking their horns and inching forward. There was this one guy in a silver pickup truck last week, he just kept creeping up, almost ran right over my toes. People are always in such a rush these days, nobody has any patience. They just want to get to where they're going, usually to grab a coffee or something. I used to drink coffee, but it made my stomach upset, so now I just stick to tea. Earl Grey is my favorite, especially with a little splash of milk. My grandmother used to drink Earl Grey every afternoon at exactly 3:00 PM. She had this beautiful porcelain tea set with little blue flowers painted on it. She said she brought it all the way from England when she moved here. I always wondered how it didn't break on the ship. But yeah, no, I don't lose awareness. I just can't remember what I'm supposed to be doing.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes when your mind goes blank, do you completely lose consciousness or awareness, even briefly?\n\nPatient: No, I don't pass out or anything. I don't lose consciousness. I'm awake the whole time. It's just... I'm just standing there, holding my sign, and my brain is completely empty. It's like someone pulled the plug on my thoughts. You know, speaking of plugs, the outlet in my bathroom has been acting up lately. I plug in my hairdryer, and it just sputters and dies. I think it's a blown fuse, but I'm terrified of messing with the fuse box. It's down in the basement, and it's so dark and damp down there. I always feel like there are spiders watching me. I hate spiders. When I was little, I found a huge wolf spider in my shoe. I had already put my foot halfway in before I felt it squirming. I screamed so loud my dad thought I was being murdered. He came running in with a baseball bat. He used to play in a softball league on the weekends, they had these bright yellow jerseys. But, um... no, I don't lose consciousness at all. I'm totally awake, just... blank.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious, upset, or experience any strong emotions when your mind goes blank?\n\nPatient: I don't... no, I don't think I feel anxious. Or upset. It's not like a panic attack or anything. I don't feel much of anything, really, just empty. It's more frustrating than upsetting, you know? Because I'm just standing there. Standing there in my bright orange vest. They make us wear these vests that are so incredibly bright, it's like a neon highlighter. I guess it's for safety, but they get so dirty so fast. The dirt from the road just clings to the reflective stripes. I tried washing mine with bleach once, but it completely ruined the reflective part. It just turned this weird, dull gray color. My supervisor was so mad at me. He's this short guy, always chewing on a toothpick. I don't know how he doesn't accidentally swallow it. He told me I had to pay for a replacement vest out of my own pocket, which felt really unfair. I mean, I was just trying to keep it clean. It's hard to keep things clean when you work outside all day. The dust just gets everywhere. It gets in your hair, under your fingernails... I spend half my evening just scrubbing my hands with that pumice soap. It smells like oranges, which is nice, but it dries my skin out terribly. I have to use so much lotion before bed. I found this really good lavender one at the pharmacy last week. It was on sale, buy one get one half off. I love a good sale. But, um... no, I don't feel any strong emotions when it happens. Just... nothing.\n\nDoctor: After your mind goes blank, do you ever find yourself doing things automatically, like moving around or fumbling with objects, without being aware of it?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I do anything like that. I just stand there, totally frozen. I don't fumble with my sign or pace around or anything. I'm just still as a statue. Speaking of statues, there's this really old bronze one in the park near my house. It's of some general from the Civil War, I think, sitting on a horse. The horse has one leg raised, which someone told me means the rider died of wounds from a battle, but I'm not sure if that's actually true. People make up all sorts of rules about things like that. Anyway, the statue is completely covered in pigeon droppings. It's actually kind of sad. I don't know why the city doesn't send someone to power wash it. They spend all this money planting new tulips every spring—red and yellow ones, mostly—but they can't be bothered to clean the statues. The tulips are beautiful, though. I tried growing tulips in my window box once, but the squirrels dug up all the bulbs before they even sprouted. Squirrels are so destructive. They chewed right through the screen on my porch last summer just to get to a bag of birdseed I'd left out. But yeah, no, I don't move around or do things automatically when my mind goes blank. I'm just stuck there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever noticed any trouble speaking, slurred speech, or any weakness or clumsiness in your arms or legs during or just after these episodes when your mind goes blank?\n\nPatient: No, I don't... my speech is normal. I don't get clumsy or weak in my arms or legs. I mean, I'm not a clumsy person in general, except for maybe when it snows. Last winter, the ice was so bad on my driveway, I slipped and tore a hole right through my favorite jeans. I was wearing these heavy snow boots, the ones with the faux fur lining at the top? They keep my feet so warm, but they make my feet look absolutely massive, like I'm walking around in cinderblocks. I actually bought them at this little outlet mall a few towns over. It's kind of sad, half the stores in that mall are boarded up now. They used to have this amazing soft pretzel stand right in the middle of the food court. The cinnamon sugar ones were the absolute best, especially when they were fresh out of the oven. The butter would just melt everywhere, you had to grab a huge stack of napkins before you even sat down. And they always had those cheap brown paper napkins that don't actually absorb anything, you know? They just smear the grease around your hands. It drove me crazy. But, um... what was I saying? Oh, right. No, my arms and legs are totally fine, no weakness or slurring or anything like that. Just the blankness.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any seizures or experienced convulsions, like shaking or jerking movements, when your mind goes blank?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have seizures or... I don't shake or jerk around. I mean, unless it's just freezing outside. Sometimes the wind down on 4th street is so bad, it makes my whole body shake while I'm holding the stop paddle. They actually gave us these new paddles last month. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, how long do they last... I'm not really sure about the exact time, maybe just a few minutes? But it feels like forever when it happens. Like, I'll be looking at the road-closure forms, or holding the stop-slow paddle, and suddenly I just cannot remember the steps I've done for years. My mind is just... gone. It's actually why I came in to see you. It's terrifying when you're standing in the middle of a work zone and you just blank out. 

Work zones are so dangerous anyway. People do not pay attention to the cones. Just last week, this guy in a silver Honda Civic drove right through the barrier tape. He wasn't even looking at the road, he was looking down at his phone, probably texting or scrolling through something. I don't understand how people can just stare at their phones while driving a two-ton machine. I read an article once that said texting and driving is just as bad as drunk driving. My uncle got a DUI back in the 90s, and it completely ruined his life. He lost his license, lost his job at the lumber yard... he used to carve these beautiful little wooden bears for me and my sister when we were little. He had this special pocket knife he used just for carving. It had a bone handle, I think? Or maybe it was ivory, which I guess is illegal now. You can't even sell ivory at antique shows anymore without all this crazy paperwork. I used to love going to antique shows with my mom on the weekends. We'd walk around for hours looking at old teacups and silver spoons... 

Wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, how long it lasts. Yeah, I don't know, maybe a few minutes? But I just completely lose my train of thought and forget how to do my job.

I can't even remember if I'm supposed to turn the sign to 'stop' or 'slow'. And it's not like I'm getting older, I'm only 41, you know? My mom started forgetting things when she was in her seventies, but she also had this terrible habit of leaving the stove on. She would make this amazing chicken soup, though. She'd boil the whole chicken for hours with carrots and celery. I tried to make it last week, but I couldn't remember if she put parsley or dill in it. I think I bought dill at the grocery store, but the cashier was so rude, she didn't even look at me when she handed me my receipt. But yeah, the road-closure forms... Like, on Tuesdays and Thursdays, I always work the intersection by the new elementary school. I always wonder how they keep them clean with all those kids around. They must have to hire those guys with the scaffolding, you know, the ones who wash skyscraper windows? I saw a documentary about them once on PBS. It looks absolutely terrifying, just dangling up there with a squeegee and a bucket. My cousin Mark used to wash windows, but just for regular storefronts downtown. He quit because he said the ammonia in the cleaner was drying out his hands too much. No, I can talk fine, it's just... the thoughts just vanish from my head.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever suddenly forgotten who you are, or been unable to remember recent events or form new memories during these episodes, even though you know where you are and what day it is?\n\nPatient: No, I always know who I am. I don't forget my name or anything like that. It's just the blanking out on what I'm doing. Like I said, I'll be out there on the road, and I just... I forget the steps. But I always know it's me standing there in the rain. Speaking of rain, my boots were absolutely soaked yesterday. I thought they were waterproof, the box said '100% waterproof guaranteed,' but they definitely aren't. I got them at that outdoor supply place out by the highway, the one with the huge stuffed bear in the lobby? It's kind of creepy, honestly. Its glass eyes just stare right at you when you walk in. I don't know why places do that, taxidermy just makes me sad. My uncle used to hunt deer and he had a giant head mounted right above his fireplace. Every Thanksgiving we'd have to sit there and eat turkey with this deer looking down at us. He always made the driest turkey, too. He'd refuse to use a roasting bag or baste it, just shoved it in the oven for five hours until it was like chewing on sawdust. We had to drown it in gravy just to swallow it. He made his gravy from a packet, which I always thought was cheating if you're going to go through all the trouble of cooking a whole bird... but yeah, no, I don't forget who I am or anything. Just the blanking out.\n\nDoctor: When your mind goes blank, do you feel like you're still fully aware of what's happening around you, or does everything seem distant or unreal, like you're watching yourself from outside your body?\n\nPatient: No, I don't feel like I'm outside my body or anything. I'm definitely still there, just... empty. Everything around me is still happening. I can hear the cars, the wind... especially the wind. It's been so windy lately. The other day, it blew my neighbor's recycling bin all the way down the street, and all these empty soda cans were just rolling everywhere. He drinks so much Diet Coke, it's unbelievable. I tried telling him about the aspartame, you know, how bad it is for you, but he just laughed and said it's his only vice. He's a really nice guy, though, always shovels my driveway when it snows. He has one of those really nice snowblowers, the red ones with the heated handles. I've always wanted one of those. Shoveling by hand is just so hard on my back, especially since I'm on my feet all day at work anyway. But yeah, no, things don't seem distant or unreal. I'm just standing there, and my brain is just... off.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any numbness, tingling, weakness, or difficulty moving any part of your body during or around the time your mind goes blank?\n\nPatient: No, I don't get any numbness or tingling. My body works fine, it's just my brain that stops. I mean, my feet ache, but that's just from standing on the pavement for hours. You really need good insoles if you're going to be a crossing guard. I bought these gel ones last month from the pharmacy, the one on the corner of 5th and Main? They have this huge display of them right when you walk in. I actually went in there just to get some cough drops because the exhaust fumes from the cars were making my throat scratchy. They had cherry and honey-lemon, and I always pick honey-lemon because cherry reminds me of that awful liquid medicine my mom used to make me take when I was a kid. It came in this dark brown glass bottle and tasted like thick, sweet plastic. I used to try and spit it into the sink when she turned around, but she always caught me because it stained the porcelain bright red. It took forever to scrub out, she had to use bleach and everything. The smell of bleach always gives me a headache now. But, um... no, no weakness or anything like that. Just the blanking out.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever noticed any changes in your vision, like blurring, double vision, or parts of your visual field missing, when your mind goes blank?\n\nPatient: No, my vision is fine. I don't get blurry or see double or anything like that. I can see everything perfectly clear, right down to the little pebbles on the street. Actually, the other day I was looking down and I saw this shiny thing in the gutter, and I thought it was a quarter. I bent down to pick it up, and it was just a stupid bottle cap. A silver one, from one of those fancy glass soda bottles. People just throw their trash everywhere, it's so disrespectful. The litter by the crosswalk is terrible. I'm always picking up fast food wrappers and empty cigarette packs. The worst is the chewing gum, though. It gets stuck to the bottom of my boots, those same boots I was telling you about, and it's impossible to scrape off. I tried using a butter knife once, and I ended up snapping the handle right off. It was one of my good knives, too, from the set my sister got me for my birthday a few years ago. She always buys me kitchen stuff, even though I don't really cook that much. I mostly just microwave things or make sandwiches. I make a really good grilled cheese, though. The secret is to use mayonnaise on the outside of the bread instead of butter. It makes it so much crispier. My mom taught me that trick. She used to make us grilled cheese and tomato soup every time it rained. But... yeah, no, my vision doesn't change at all when my mind stops. I can see just fine.\n\nDoctor: While your mind is blank, do you lose awareness of what’s happening around you, or does everything remain clear and understandable even though your thoughts are gone?\n\nPatient: Well, like I said, everything around me is still clear. I'm aware of it all. It's just my thoughts that vanish. That's actually why I came in, because my mind keeps going blank at the worst times. Like, I'll look at the road-closure forms or my stop-slow paddle, and I suddenly cannot remember the steps I've done for years. It's so embarrassing. Especially with the paddle, because the drivers are looking right at you, waiting to see if they can go. Some of them get so impatient, honking their horns and inching forward. There was this one guy in a silver pickup truck last week, he just kept creeping up, almost ran right over my toes. People are always in such a rush these days, nobody has any patience. They just want to get to where they're going, usually to grab a coffee or something. I used to drink coffee, but it made my stomach upset, so now I just stick to tea. Earl Grey is my favorite, especially with a little splash of milk. My grandmother used to drink Earl Grey every afternoon at exactly 3:00 PM. She had this beautiful porcelain tea set with little blue flowers painted on it. She said she brought it all the way from England when she moved here. I always wondered how it didn't break on the ship. But yeah, no, I don't lose awareness. I just can't remember what I'm supposed to be doing.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes when your mind goes blank, do you completely lose consciousness or awareness, even briefly?\n\nPatient: No, I don't pass out or anything. I don't lose consciousness. I'm awake the whole time. It's just... I'm just standing there, holding my sign, and my brain is completely empty. It's like someone pulled the plug on my thoughts. You know, speaking of plugs, the outlet in my bathroom has been acting up lately. I plug in my hairdryer, and it just sputters and dies. I think it's a blown fuse, but I'm terrified of messing with the fuse box. It's down in the basement, and it's so dark and damp down there. I always feel like there are spiders watching me. I hate spiders. When I was little, I found a huge wolf spider in my shoe. I had already put my foot halfway in before I felt it squirming. I screamed so loud my dad thought I was being murdered. He came running in with a baseball bat. He used to play in a softball league on the weekends, they had these bright yellow jerseys. But, um... no, I don't lose consciousness at all. I'm totally awake, just... blank.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious, upset, or experience any strong emotions when your mind goes blank?\n\nPatient: I don't... no, I don't think I feel anxious. Or upset. It's not like a panic attack or anything. I don't feel much of anything, really, just empty. It's more frustrating than upsetting, you know? Because I'm just standing there. Standing there in my bright orange vest. They make us wear these vests that are so incredibly bright, it's like a neon highlighter. I guess it's for safety, but they get so dirty so fast. The dirt from the road just clings to the reflective stripes. I tried washing mine with bleach once, but it completely ruined the reflective part. It just turned this weird, dull gray color. My supervisor was so mad at me. He's this short guy, always chewing on a toothpick. I don't know how he doesn't accidentally swallow it. He told me I had to pay for a replacement vest out of my own pocket, which felt really unfair. I mean, I was just trying to keep it clean. It's hard to keep things clean when you work outside all day. The dust just gets everywhere. It gets in your hair, under your fingernails... I spend half my evening just scrubbing my hands with that pumice soap. It smells like oranges, which is nice, but it dries my skin out terribly. I have to use so much lotion before bed. I found this really good lavender one at the pharmacy last week. It was on sale, buy one get one half off. I love a good sale. But, um... no, I don't feel any strong emotions when it happens. Just... nothing.\n\nDoctor: After your mind goes blank, do you ever find yourself doing things automatically, like moving around or fumbling with objects, without being aware of it?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I do anything like that. I just stand there, totally frozen. I don't fumble with my sign or pace around or anything. I'm just still as a statue. Speaking of statues, there's this really old bronze one in the park near my house. It's of some general from the Civil War, I think, sitting on a horse. The horse has one leg raised, which someone told me means the rider died of wounds from a battle, but I'm not sure if that's actually true. People make up all sorts of rules about things like that. Anyway, the statue is completely covered in pigeon droppings. It's actually kind of sad. I don't know why the city doesn't send someone to power wash it. They spend all this money planting new tulips every spring—red and yellow ones, mostly—but they can't be bothered to clean the statues. The tulips are beautiful, though. I tried growing tulips in my window box once, but the squirrels dug up all the bulbs before they even sprouted. Squirrels are so destructive. They chewed right through the screen on my porch last summer just to get to a bag of birdseed I'd left out. But yeah, no, I don't move around or do things automatically when my mind goes blank. I'm just stuck there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever noticed any trouble speaking, slurred speech, or any weakness or clumsiness in your arms or legs during or just after these episodes when your mind goes blank?\n\nPatient: No, I don't... my speech is normal. I don't get clumsy or weak in my arms or legs. I mean, I'm not a clumsy person in general, except for maybe when it snows. Last winter, the ice was so bad on my driveway, I slipped and tore a hole right through my favorite jeans. I was wearing these heavy snow boots, the ones with the faux fur lining at the top? They keep my feet so warm, but they make my feet look absolutely massive, like I'm walking around in cinderblocks. I actually bought them at this little outlet mall a few towns over. It's kind of sad, half the stores in that mall are boarded up now. They used to have this amazing soft pretzel stand right in the middle of the food court. The cinnamon sugar ones were the absolute best, especially when they were fresh out of the oven. The butter would just melt everywhere, you had to grab a huge stack of napkins before you even sat down. And they always had those cheap brown paper napkins that don't actually absorb anything, you know? They just smear the grease around your hands. It drove me crazy. But, um... what was I saying? Oh, right. No, my arms and legs are totally fine, no weakness or slurring or anything like that. Just the blankness.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any seizures or experienced convulsions, like shaking or jerking movements, when your mind goes blank?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have seizures or... I don't shake or jerk around. I mean, unless it's just freezing outside. Sometimes the wind down on 4th street is so bad, it makes my whole body shake while I'm holding the stop paddle. They actually gave us these new paddles last month. They're supposed to be lighter, made of some kind of aerospace aluminum or something. \n\nMy brother used to be obsessed with airplanes when we were kids. He built all these little model planes and hung them from his bedroom ceiling with fishing line. They looked really neat, actually, until our cat got into his room and jumped off the top of the bookshelf, trying to catch a little B-17 bomber. Brought the whole ceiling hook down and left this massive hole in the drywall. My dad tried to patch it himself with spackle, but he didn't sand it down right, so there was just this ugly, crusty lump on the ceiling for years. It drove my mom crazy. She finally just painted over it, but you could still see the shadow of the lump when the afternoon sun hit it through the blinds. She always kept those blinds drawn because the neighbor's porch light was way too bright, it was one of those harsh LED ones that just blinds you if you look right at it... \n\nBut, um, what were you asking? Oh. No, no convulsions or anything. Just the blanking out.\n\nDoctor: How long do these episodes of your mind going blank usually last?\n\nPatient: Um, how long do they last... I'm not really sure about the exact time, maybe just a few minutes? But it feels like forever when it happens. Like, I'll be looking at the road-closure forms, or holding the stop-slow paddle, and suddenly I just cannot remember the steps I've done for years. My mind is just... gone. It's actually why I came in to see you. It's terrifying when you're standing in the middle of a work zone and you just blank out. \n\nWork zones are so dangerous anyway. People do not pay attention to the cones. Just last week, this guy in a silver Honda Civic drove right through the barrier tape. He wasn't even looking at the road, he was looking down at his phone, probably texting or scrolling through something. I don't understand how people can just stare at their phones while driving a two-ton machine. I read an article once that said texting and driving is just as bad as drunk driving. My uncle got a DUI back in the 90s, and it completely ruined his life. He lost his license, lost his job at the lumber yard... he used to carve these beautiful little wooden bears for me and my sister when we were little. He had this special pocket knife he used just for carving. It had a bone handle, I think? Or maybe it was ivory, which I guess is illegal now. You can't even sell ivory at antique shows anymore without all this crazy paperwork. I used to love going to antique shows with my mom on the weekends. We'd walk around for hours looking at old teacups and silver spoons... \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, how long it lasts. Yeah, I don't know, maybe a few minutes? But I just completely lose my train of thought and forget how to do my job.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

My mom started forgetting things when she was in her seventies, but she also had this terrible habit of leaving the stove on. She would make this amazing chicken soup, though. She'd boil the whole chicken for hours with carrots and celery. I tried to make it last week, but I couldn't remember if she put parsley or dill in it. I think I bought dill at the grocery store, but the cashier was so rude, she didn't even look at me when she handed me my receipt. But yeah, the road-closure forms... It's like my thoughts are just wiped away.\n\nDoctor: When your mind goes blank, do you also feel confused about where you are, what day it is, or have trouble finding the right words when you speak?\n\nPatient: No, I... I don't think so. I mean, I know where I am. I always know what day it is because my schedule is really strict. Like, on Tuesdays and Thursdays, I always work the intersection by the new elementary school. They just finished building it last year, and honestly, the architecture is so modern it looks more like an art museum than a school. It has these huge, floor-to-ceiling glass windows in the front. I always wonder how they keep them clean with all those kids around. They must have to hire those guys with the scaffolding, you know, the ones who wash skyscraper windows? I saw a documentary about them once on PBS. It looks absolutely terrifying, just dangling up there with a squeegee and a bucket. My cousin Mark used to wash windows, but just for regular storefronts downtown. He quit because he said the ammonia in the cleaner was drying out his hands too much. He buys this really expensive shea butter lotion now from a little kiosk at the mall... but, um... wait, what were you asking? Did you ask about my words? No, I can talk fine, it's just... the thoughts just vanish from my head.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever suddenly forgotten who you are, or been unable to remember recent events or form new memories during these episodes, even though you know where you are and what day it is?\n\nPatient: No, I always know who I am. I don't forget my name or anything like that. It's just the blanking out on what I'm doing. Like I said, I'll be out there on the road, and I just... I forget the steps. But I always know it's me standing there in the rain. Speaking of rain, my boots were absolutely soaked yesterday. I thought they were waterproof, the box said '100% waterproof guaranteed,' but they definitely aren't. I got them at that outdoor supply place out by the highway, the one with the huge stuffed bear in the lobby? It's kind of creepy, honestly. Its glass eyes just stare right at you when you walk in. I don't know why places do that, taxidermy just makes me sad. My uncle used to hunt deer and he had a giant head mounted right above his fireplace. Every Thanksgiving we'd have to sit there and eat turkey with this deer looking down at us. He always made the driest turkey, too. He'd refuse to use a roasting bag or baste it, just shoved it in the oven for five hours until it was like chewing on sawdust. We had to drown it in gravy just to swallow it. He made his gravy from a packet, which I always thought was cheating if you're going to go through all the trouble of cooking a whole bird... but yeah, no, I don't forget who I am or anything. Just the blanking out.\n\nDoctor: When your mind goes blank, do you feel like you're still fully aware of what's happening around you, or does everything seem distant or unreal, like you're watching yourself from outside your body?\n\nPatient: No, I don't feel like I'm outside my body or anything. I'm definitely still there, just... empty. Everything around me is still happening. I can hear the cars, the wind... especially the wind. It's been so windy lately. The other day, it blew my neighbor's recycling bin all the way down the street, and all these empty soda cans were just rolling everywhere. He drinks so much Diet Coke, it's unbelievable. I tried telling him about the aspartame, you know, how bad it is for you, but he just laughed and said it's his only vice. He's a really nice guy, though, always shovels my driveway when it snows. He has one of those really nice snowblowers, the red ones with the heated handles. I've always wanted one of those. Shoveling by hand is just so hard on my back, especially since I'm on my feet all day at work anyway. But yeah, no, things don't seem distant or unreal. I'm just standing there, and my brain is just... off.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any numbness, tingling, weakness, or difficulty moving any part of your body during or around the time your mind goes blank?\n\nPatient: No, I don't get any numbness or tingling. My body works fine, it's just my brain that stops. I mean, my feet ache, but that's just from standing on the pavement for hours. You really need good insoles if you're going to be a crossing guard. I bought these gel ones last month from the pharmacy, the one on the corner of 5th and Main? They have this huge display of them right when you walk in. I actually went in there just to get some cough drops because the exhaust fumes from the cars were making my throat scratchy. They had cherry and honey-lemon, and I always pick honey-lemon because cherry reminds me of that awful liquid medicine my mom used to make me take when I was a kid. It came in this dark brown glass bottle and tasted like thick, sweet plastic. I used to try and spit it into the sink when she turned around, but she always caught me because it stained the porcelain bright red. It took forever to scrub out, she had to use bleach and everything. The smell of bleach always gives me a headache now. But, um... no, no weakness or anything like that. Just the blanking out.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever noticed any changes in your vision, like blurring, double vision, or parts of your visual field missing, when your mind goes blank?\n\nPatient: No, my vision is fine. I don't get blurry or see double or anything like that. I can see everything perfectly clear, right down to the little pebbles on the street. Actually, the other day I was looking down and I saw this shiny thing in the gutter, and I thought it was a quarter. I bent down to pick it up, and it was just a stupid bottle cap. A silver one, from one of those fancy glass soda bottles. People just throw their trash everywhere, it's so disrespectful. The litter by the crosswalk is terrible. I'm always picking up fast food wrappers and empty cigarette packs. The worst is the chewing gum, though. It gets stuck to the bottom of my boots, those same boots I was telling you about, and it's impossible to scrape off. I tried using a butter knife once, and I ended up snapping the handle right off. It was one of my good knives, too, from the set my sister got me for my birthday a few years ago. She always buys me kitchen stuff, even though I don't really cook that much. I mostly just microwave things or make sandwiches. I make a really good grilled cheese, though. The secret is to use mayonnaise on the outside of the bread instead of butter. It makes it so much crispier. My mom taught me that trick. She used to make us grilled cheese and tomato soup every time it rained. But... yeah, no, my vision doesn't change at all when my mind stops. I can see just fine.\n\nDoctor: While your mind is blank, do you lose awareness of what’s happening around you, or does everything remain clear and understandable even though your thoughts are gone?\n\nPatient: Well, like I said, everything around me is still clear. I'm aware of it all. It's just my thoughts that vanish. That's actually why I came in, because my mind keeps going blank at the worst times. Like, I'll look at the road-closure forms or my stop-slow paddle, and I suddenly cannot remember the steps I've done for years. It's so embarrassing. Especially with the paddle, because the drivers are looking right at you, waiting to see if they can go. Some of them get so impatient, honking their horns and inching forward. There was this one guy in a silver pickup truck last week, he just kept creeping up, almost ran right over my toes. People are always in such a rush these days, nobody has any patience. They just want to get to where they're going, usually to grab a coffee or something. I used to drink coffee, but it made my stomach upset, so now I just stick to tea. Earl Grey is my favorite, especially with a little splash of milk. My grandmother used to drink Earl Grey every afternoon at exactly 3:00 PM. She had this beautiful porcelain tea set with little blue flowers painted on it. She said she brought it all the way from England when she moved here. I always wondered how it didn't break on the ship. But yeah, no, I don't lose awareness. I just can't remember what I'm supposed to be doing.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes when your mind goes blank, do you completely lose consciousness or awareness, even briefly?\n\nPatient: No, I don't pass out or anything. I don't lose consciousness. I'm awake the whole time. It's just... I'm just standing there, holding my sign, and my brain is completely empty. It's like someone pulled the plug on my thoughts. You know, speaking of plugs, the outlet in my bathroom has been acting up lately. I plug in my hairdryer, and it just sputters and dies. I think it's a blown fuse, but I'm terrified of messing with the fuse box. It's down in the basement, and it's so dark and damp down there. I always feel like there are spiders watching me. I hate spiders. When I was little, I found a huge wolf spider in my shoe. I had already put my foot halfway in before I felt it squirming. I screamed so loud my dad thought I was being murdered. He came running in with a baseball bat. He used to play in a softball league on the weekends, they had these bright yellow jerseys. But, um... no, I don't lose consciousness at all. I'm totally awake, just... blank.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious, upset, or experience any strong emotions when your mind goes blank?\n\nPatient: I don't... no, I don't think I feel anxious. Or upset. It's not like a panic attack or anything. I don't feel much of anything, really, just empty. It's more frustrating than upsetting, you know? Because I'm just standing there. Standing there in my bright orange vest. They make us wear these vests that are so incredibly bright, it's like a neon highlighter. I guess it's for safety, but they get so dirty so fast. The dirt from the road just clings to the reflective stripes. I tried washing mine with bleach once, but it completely ruined the reflective part. It just turned this weird, dull gray color. My supervisor was so mad at me. He's this short guy, always chewing on a toothpick. I don't know how he doesn't accidentally swallow it. He told me I had to pay for a replacement vest out of my own pocket, which felt really unfair. I mean, I was just trying to keep it clean. It's hard to keep things clean when you work outside all day. The dust just gets everywhere. It gets in your hair, under your fingernails... I spend half my evening just scrubbing my hands with that pumice soap. It smells like oranges, which is nice, but it dries my skin out terribly. I have to use so much lotion before bed. I found this really good lavender one at the pharmacy last week. It was on sale, buy one get one half off. I love a good sale. But, um... no, I don't feel any strong emotions when it happens. Just... nothing.\n\nDoctor: After your mind goes blank, do you ever find yourself doing things automatically, like moving around or fumbling with objects, without being aware of it?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I do anything like that. I just stand there, totally frozen. I don't fumble with my sign or pace around or anything. I'm just still as a statue. Speaking of statues, there's this really old bronze one in the park near my house. It's of some general from the Civil War, I think, sitting on a horse. The horse has one leg raised, which someone told me means the rider died of wounds from a battle, but I'm not sure if that's actually true. People make up all sorts of rules about things like that. Anyway, the statue is completely covered in pigeon droppings. It's actually kind of sad. I don't know why the city doesn't send someone to power wash it. They spend all this money planting new tulips every spring—red and yellow ones, mostly—but they can't be bothered to clean the statues. The tulips are beautiful, though. I tried growing tulips in my window box once, but the squirrels dug up all the bulbs before they even sprouted. Squirrels are so destructive. They chewed right through the screen on my porch last summer just to get to a bag of birdseed I'd left out. But yeah, no, I don't move around or do things automatically when my mind goes blank. I'm just stuck there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever noticed any trouble speaking, slurred speech, or any weakness or clumsiness in your arms or legs during or just after these episodes when your mind goes blank?\n\nPatient: No, I don't... my speech is normal. I don't get clumsy or weak in my arms or legs. I mean, I'm not a clumsy person in general, except for maybe when it snows. Last winter, the ice was so bad on my driveway, I slipped and tore a hole right through my favorite jeans. I was wearing these heavy snow boots, the ones with the faux fur lining at the top? They keep my feet so warm, but they make my feet look absolutely massive, like I'm walking around in cinderblocks. I actually bought them at this little outlet mall a few towns over. It's kind of sad, half the stores in that mall are boarded up now. They used to have this amazing soft pretzel stand right in the middle of the food court. The cinnamon sugar ones were the absolute best, especially when they were fresh out of the oven. The butter would just melt everywhere, you had to grab a huge stack of napkins before you even sat down. And they always had those cheap brown paper napkins that don't actually absorb anything, you know? They just smear the grease around your hands. It drove me crazy. But, um... what was I saying? Oh, right. No, my arms and legs are totally fine, no weakness or slurring or anything like that. Just the blankness.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any seizures or experienced convulsions, like shaking or jerking movements, when your mind goes blank?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have seizures or... I don't shake or jerk around. I mean, unless it's just freezing outside. Sometimes the wind down on 4th street is so bad, it makes my whole body shake while I'm holding the stop paddle. They actually gave us these new paddles last month. They're supposed to be lighter, made of some kind of aerospace aluminum or something. \n\nMy brother used to be obsessed with airplanes when we were kids. He built all these little model planes and hung them from his bedroom ceiling with fishing line. They looked really neat, actually, until our cat got into his room and jumped off the top of the bookshelf, trying to catch a little B-17 bomber. Brought the whole ceiling hook down and left this massive hole in the drywall. My dad tried to patch it himself with spackle, but he didn't sand it down right, so there was just this ugly, crusty lump on the ceiling for years. It drove my mom crazy. She finally just painted over it, but you could still see the shadow of the lump when the afternoon sun hit it through the blinds. She always kept those blinds drawn because the neighbor's porch light was way too bright, it was one of those harsh LED ones that just blinds you if you look right at it... \n\nBut, um, what were you asking? Oh. No, no convulsions or anything. Just the blanking out.\n\nDoctor: How long do these episodes of your mind going blank usually last?\n\nPatient: Um, how long do they last... I'm not really sure about the exact time, maybe just a few minutes? But it feels like forever when it happens. Like, I'll be looking at the road-closure forms, or holding the stop-slow paddle, and suddenly I just cannot remember the steps I've done for years. My mind is just... gone. It's actually why I came in to see you. It's terrifying when you're standing in the middle of a work zone and you just blank out. \n\nWork zones are so dangerous anyway. People do not pay attention to the cones. Just last week, this guy in a silver Honda Civic drove right through the barrier tape. He wasn't even looking at the road, he was looking down at his phone, probably texting or scrolling through something. I don't understand how people can just stare at their phones while driving a two-ton machine. I read an article once that said texting and driving is just as bad as drunk driving. My uncle got a DUI back in the 90s, and it completely ruined his life. He lost his license, lost his job at the lumber yard... he used to carve these beautiful little wooden bears for me and my sister when we were little. He had this special pocket knife he used just for carving. It had a bone handle, I think? Or maybe it was ivory, which I guess is illegal now. You can't even sell ivory at antique shows anymore without all this crazy paperwork. I used to love going to antique shows with my mom on the weekends. We'd walk around for hours looking at old teacups and silver spoons... \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, how long it lasts. Yeah, I don't know, maybe a few minutes? But I just completely lose my train of thought and forget how to do my job.\n\nDoctor: After your mind comes back online, do you feel confused, have trouble remembering what happened, or find it hard to get back to what you were doing?\n\nPatient: Um, when I come back... no, not really confused. I mean, I know where I am and everything. It's just getting back into the rhythm of the job that's hard. Like, I have to figure out where I left off. But I don't forget what happened, I just... pick up the pieces. \n\nSpeaking of pieces, I was trying to put together this 1000-piece puzzle the other night. It was a picture of this old European castle, you know, the ones with the really pointy turrets? It was supposed to be relaxing, but all the sky pieces were the exact same shade of blue. I swear, they cut them all exactly the same shape, too. I spent three hours just trying to do the top left corner. My cat kept jumping on the table and knocking the pieces onto the rug. He's an orange tabby, and he blends right into that rug. It's this ugly burnt orange shag carpet from the 70s that came with the apartment. I've been meaning to ask the landlord if I can rip it up, because I'm pretty sure there's hardwood underneath. I saw a corner peeling up by the radiator. Radiators are so loud in the winter, mine clanks and hisses like it's going to explode. It wakes me up at 3 AM every single night. \n\nBut, um... sorry, what was the question? Oh, yeah, no, I don't feel confused afterwards. Just takes a second to get back to what I was doing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Um, understanding people? No, I don't think so. I mean, if someone talks to me, I hear them fine. And I can talk back, it's not like my mouth stops working. It's just my brain that sort of pauses. 

I guess it's like when you hit pause on the TV. I was watching this documentary last night about deep sea creatures, the ones that live way down where there's no light at all. My cousin Mark used to wash windows, but just for regular storefronts downtown. He quit because he said the ammonia in the cleaner was drying out his hands too much. He buys this really expensive shea butter lotion now from a little kiosk at the mall... but, um... wait, what were you asking? Did you ask about my words? No, I can talk fine, it's just... the thoughts just vanish from my head.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever suddenly forgotten who you are, or been unable to remember recent events or form new memories during these episodes, even though you know where you are and what day it is?\n\nPatient: No, I always know who I am. I don't forget my name or anything like that. It's just the blanking out on what I'm doing. Like I said, I'll be out there on the road, and I just... I forget the steps. But I always know it's me standing there in the rain. Speaking of rain, my boots were absolutely soaked yesterday. I thought they were waterproof, the box said '100% waterproof guaranteed,' but they definitely aren't. I got them at that outdoor supply place out by the highway, the one with the huge stuffed bear in the lobby? It's kind of creepy, honestly. Its glass eyes just stare right at you when you walk in. I don't know why places do that, taxidermy just makes me sad. My uncle used to hunt deer and he had a giant head mounted right above his fireplace. Every Thanksgiving we'd have to sit there and eat turkey with this deer looking down at us. He always made the driest turkey, too. He'd refuse to use a roasting bag or baste it, just shoved it in the oven for five hours until it was like chewing on sawdust. We had to drown it in gravy just to swallow it. He made his gravy from a packet, which I always thought was cheating if you're going to go through all the trouble of cooking a whole bird... but yeah, no, I don't forget who I am or anything. Just the blanking out.\n\nDoctor: When your mind goes blank, do you feel like you're still fully aware of what's happening around you, or does everything seem distant or unreal, like you're watching yourself from outside your body?\n\nPatient: No, I don't feel like I'm outside my body or anything. I'm definitely still there, just... empty. Everything around me is still happening. I can hear the cars, the wind... especially the wind. It's been so windy lately. The other day, it blew my neighbor's recycling bin all the way down the street, and all these empty soda cans were just rolling everywhere. He drinks so much Diet Coke, it's unbelievable. I tried telling him about the aspartame, you know, how bad it is for you, but he just laughed and said it's his only vice. He's a really nice guy, though, always shovels my driveway when it snows. He has one of those really nice snowblowers, the red ones with the heated handles. I've always wanted one of those. Shoveling by hand is just so hard on my back, especially since I'm on my feet all day at work anyway. But yeah, no, things don't seem distant or unreal. I'm just standing there, and my brain is just... off.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any numbness, tingling, weakness, or difficulty moving any part of your body during or around the time your mind goes blank?\n\nPatient: No, I don't get any numbness or tingling. My body works fine, it's just my brain that stops. I mean, my feet ache, but that's just from standing on the pavement for hours. You really need good insoles if you're going to be a crossing guard. I bought these gel ones last month from the pharmacy, the one on the corner of 5th and Main? They have this huge display of them right when you walk in. I actually went in there just to get some cough drops because the exhaust fumes from the cars were making my throat scratchy. They had cherry and honey-lemon, and I always pick honey-lemon because cherry reminds me of that awful liquid medicine my mom used to make me take when I was a kid. It came in this dark brown glass bottle and tasted like thick, sweet plastic. I used to try and spit it into the sink when she turned around, but she always caught me because it stained the porcelain bright red. It took forever to scrub out, she had to use bleach and everything. The smell of bleach always gives me a headache now. But, um... no, no weakness or anything like that. Just the blanking out.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever noticed any changes in your vision, like blurring, double vision, or parts of your visual field missing, when your mind goes blank?\n\nPatient: No, my vision is fine. I don't get blurry or see double or anything like that. I can see everything perfectly clear, right down to the little pebbles on the street. Actually, the other day I was looking down and I saw this shiny thing in the gutter, and I thought it was a quarter. I bent down to pick it up, and it was just a stupid bottle cap. A silver one, from one of those fancy glass soda bottles. People just throw their trash everywhere, it's so disrespectful. The litter by the crosswalk is terrible. I'm always picking up fast food wrappers and empty cigarette packs. The worst is the chewing gum, though. It gets stuck to the bottom of my boots, those same boots I was telling you about, and it's impossible to scrape off. I tried using a butter knife once, and I ended up snapping the handle right off. It was one of my good knives, too, from the set my sister got me for my birthday a few years ago. She always buys me kitchen stuff, even though I don't really cook that much. I mostly just microwave things or make sandwiches. I make a really good grilled cheese, though. The secret is to use mayonnaise on the outside of the bread instead of butter. It makes it so much crispier. My mom taught me that trick. She used to make us grilled cheese and tomato soup every time it rained. But... yeah, no, my vision doesn't change at all when my mind stops. I can see just fine.\n\nDoctor: While your mind is blank, do you lose awareness of what’s happening around you, or does everything remain clear and understandable even though your thoughts are gone?\n\nPatient: Well, like I said, everything around me is still clear. I'm aware of it all. It's just my thoughts that vanish. That's actually why I came in, because my mind keeps going blank at the worst times. Like, I'll look at the road-closure forms or my stop-slow paddle, and I suddenly cannot remember the steps I've done for years. It's so embarrassing. Especially with the paddle, because the drivers are looking right at you, waiting to see if they can go. Some of them get so impatient, honking their horns and inching forward. There was this one guy in a silver pickup truck last week, he just kept creeping up, almost ran right over my toes. People are always in such a rush these days, nobody has any patience. They just want to get to where they're going, usually to grab a coffee or something. I used to drink coffee, but it made my stomach upset, so now I just stick to tea. Earl Grey is my favorite, especially with a little splash of milk. My grandmother used to drink Earl Grey every afternoon at exactly 3:00 PM. She had this beautiful porcelain tea set with little blue flowers painted on it. She said she brought it all the way from England when she moved here. I always wondered how it didn't break on the ship. But yeah, no, I don't lose awareness. I just can't remember what I'm supposed to be doing.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes when your mind goes blank, do you completely lose consciousness or awareness, even briefly?\n\nPatient: No, I don't pass out or anything. I don't lose consciousness. I'm awake the whole time. It's just... I'm just standing there, holding my sign, and my brain is completely empty. It's like someone pulled the plug on my thoughts. You know, speaking of plugs, the outlet in my bathroom has been acting up lately. I plug in my hairdryer, and it just sputters and dies. I think it's a blown fuse, but I'm terrified of messing with the fuse box. It's down in the basement, and it's so dark and damp down there. I always feel like there are spiders watching me. I hate spiders. When I was little, I found a huge wolf spider in my shoe. I had already put my foot halfway in before I felt it squirming. I screamed so loud my dad thought I was being murdered. He came running in with a baseball bat. He used to play in a softball league on the weekends, they had these bright yellow jerseys. But, um... no, I don't lose consciousness at all. I'm totally awake, just... blank.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious, upset, or experience any strong emotions when your mind goes blank?\n\nPatient: I don't... no, I don't think I feel anxious. Or upset. It's not like a panic attack or anything. I don't feel much of anything, really, just empty. It's more frustrating than upsetting, you know? Because I'm just standing there. Standing there in my bright orange vest. They make us wear these vests that are so incredibly bright, it's like a neon highlighter. I guess it's for safety, but they get so dirty so fast. The dirt from the road just clings to the reflective stripes. I tried washing mine with bleach once, but it completely ruined the reflective part. It just turned this weird, dull gray color. My supervisor was so mad at me. He's this short guy, always chewing on a toothpick. I don't know how he doesn't accidentally swallow it. He told me I had to pay for a replacement vest out of my own pocket, which felt really unfair. I mean, I was just trying to keep it clean. It's hard to keep things clean when you work outside all day. The dust just gets everywhere. It gets in your hair, under your fingernails... I spend half my evening just scrubbing my hands with that pumice soap. It smells like oranges, which is nice, but it dries my skin out terribly. I have to use so much lotion before bed. I found this really good lavender one at the pharmacy last week. It was on sale, buy one get one half off. I love a good sale. But, um... no, I don't feel any strong emotions when it happens. Just... nothing.\n\nDoctor: After your mind goes blank, do you ever find yourself doing things automatically, like moving around or fumbling with objects, without being aware of it?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I do anything like that. I just stand there, totally frozen. I don't fumble with my sign or pace around or anything. I'm just still as a statue. Speaking of statues, there's this really old bronze one in the park near my house. It's of some general from the Civil War, I think, sitting on a horse. The horse has one leg raised, which someone told me means the rider died of wounds from a battle, but I'm not sure if that's actually true. People make up all sorts of rules about things like that. Anyway, the statue is completely covered in pigeon droppings. It's actually kind of sad. I don't know why the city doesn't send someone to power wash it. They spend all this money planting new tulips every spring—red and yellow ones, mostly—but they can't be bothered to clean the statues. The tulips are beautiful, though. I tried growing tulips in my window box once, but the squirrels dug up all the bulbs before they even sprouted. Squirrels are so destructive. They chewed right through the screen on my porch last summer just to get to a bag of birdseed I'd left out. But yeah, no, I don't move around or do things automatically when my mind goes blank. I'm just stuck there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever noticed any trouble speaking, slurred speech, or any weakness or clumsiness in your arms or legs during or just after these episodes when your mind goes blank?\n\nPatient: No, I don't... my speech is normal. I don't get clumsy or weak in my arms or legs. I mean, I'm not a clumsy person in general, except for maybe when it snows. Last winter, the ice was so bad on my driveway, I slipped and tore a hole right through my favorite jeans. I was wearing these heavy snow boots, the ones with the faux fur lining at the top? They keep my feet so warm, but they make my feet look absolutely massive, like I'm walking around in cinderblocks. I actually bought them at this little outlet mall a few towns over. It's kind of sad, half the stores in that mall are boarded up now. They used to have this amazing soft pretzel stand right in the middle of the food court. The cinnamon sugar ones were the absolute best, especially when they were fresh out of the oven. The butter would just melt everywhere, you had to grab a huge stack of napkins before you even sat down. And they always had those cheap brown paper napkins that don't actually absorb anything, you know? They just smear the grease around your hands. It drove me crazy. But, um... what was I saying? Oh, right. No, my arms and legs are totally fine, no weakness or slurring or anything like that. Just the blankness.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any seizures or experienced convulsions, like shaking or jerking movements, when your mind goes blank?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have seizures or... I don't shake or jerk around. I mean, unless it's just freezing outside. Sometimes the wind down on 4th street is so bad, it makes my whole body shake while I'm holding the stop paddle. They actually gave us these new paddles last month. They're supposed to be lighter, made of some kind of aerospace aluminum or something. \n\nMy brother used to be obsessed with airplanes when we were kids. He built all these little model planes and hung them from his bedroom ceiling with fishing line. They looked really neat, actually, until our cat got into his room and jumped off the top of the bookshelf, trying to catch a little B-17 bomber. Brought the whole ceiling hook down and left this massive hole in the drywall. My dad tried to patch it himself with spackle, but he didn't sand it down right, so there was just this ugly, crusty lump on the ceiling for years. It drove my mom crazy. She finally just painted over it, but you could still see the shadow of the lump when the afternoon sun hit it through the blinds. She always kept those blinds drawn because the neighbor's porch light was way too bright, it was one of those harsh LED ones that just blinds you if you look right at it... \n\nBut, um, what were you asking? Oh. No, no convulsions or anything. Just the blanking out.\n\nDoctor: How long do these episodes of your mind going blank usually last?\n\nPatient: Um, how long do they last... I'm not really sure about the exact time, maybe just a few minutes? But it feels like forever when it happens. Like, I'll be looking at the road-closure forms, or holding the stop-slow paddle, and suddenly I just cannot remember the steps I've done for years. My mind is just... gone. It's actually why I came in to see you. It's terrifying when you're standing in the middle of a work zone and you just blank out. \n\nWork zones are so dangerous anyway. People do not pay attention to the cones. Just last week, this guy in a silver Honda Civic drove right through the barrier tape. He wasn't even looking at the road, he was looking down at his phone, probably texting or scrolling through something. I don't understand how people can just stare at their phones while driving a two-ton machine. I read an article once that said texting and driving is just as bad as drunk driving. My uncle got a DUI back in the 90s, and it completely ruined his life. He lost his license, lost his job at the lumber yard... he used to carve these beautiful little wooden bears for me and my sister when we were little. He had this special pocket knife he used just for carving. It had a bone handle, I think? Or maybe it was ivory, which I guess is illegal now. You can't even sell ivory at antique shows anymore without all this crazy paperwork. I used to love going to antique shows with my mom on the weekends. We'd walk around for hours looking at old teacups and silver spoons... \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, how long it lasts. Yeah, I don't know, maybe a few minutes? But I just completely lose my train of thought and forget how to do my job.\n\nDoctor: After your mind comes back online, do you feel confused, have trouble remembering what happened, or find it hard to get back to what you were doing?\n\nPatient: Um, when I come back... no, not really confused. I mean, I know where I am and everything. It's just getting back into the rhythm of the job that's hard. Like, I have to figure out where I left off. But I don't forget what happened, I just... pick up the pieces. \n\nSpeaking of pieces, I was trying to put together this 1000-piece puzzle the other night. It was a picture of this old European castle, you know, the ones with the really pointy turrets? It was supposed to be relaxing, but all the sky pieces were the exact same shade of blue. I swear, they cut them all exactly the same shape, too. I spent three hours just trying to do the top left corner. My cat kept jumping on the table and knocking the pieces onto the rug. He's an orange tabby, and he blends right into that rug. It's this ugly burnt orange shag carpet from the 70s that came with the apartment. I've been meaning to ask the landlord if I can rip it up, because I'm pretty sure there's hardwood underneath. I saw a corner peeling up by the radiator. Radiators are so loud in the winter, mine clanks and hisses like it's going to explode. It wakes me up at 3 AM every single night. \n\nBut, um... sorry, what was the question? Oh, yeah, no, I don't feel confused afterwards. Just takes a second to get back to what I was doing.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes when your mind goes blank, do you ever have trouble understanding what people are saying to you or find yourself unable to speak even though you want to?\n\nPatient: Um, understanding people? No, I don't think so. I mean, if someone talks to me, I hear them fine. And I can talk back, it's not like my mouth stops working. It's just my brain that sort of pauses. \n\nI guess it's like when you hit pause on the TV. I was watching this documentary last night about deep sea creatures, the ones that live way down where there's no light at all. They have these weird glowing parts on their bodies, bioluminescence, I think they called it. It's amazing how they can survive under all that pressure. The narrator had this really deep, soothing voice, kind of like Morgan Freeman, but not him. I love Morgan Freeman's voice. He played the president in that one movie about the comet hitting the earth. I always wonder what I would do if a comet was coming. Probably just pack up my cat and drive to the mountains. I went camping in the mountains once when I was in college. We forgot the tent poles and had to sleep in the car. It was a tiny little hatchback, a Ford Fiesta, I think. My legs were so cramped the next morning I could barely walk. \n\nBut, um... sorry, what were we talking about? Oh, right, talking during the blank spells. Yeah, no, I can speak fine, I just... I just lose my place in what I'm doing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
